### **@wuwu365** | 👍 3 likes
[View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=YXTRlvFIRt8&lc=Ugz7BDPxDwLNXUpgfzt4AaABAg)

本次演講深入剖析了古羅馬詩人維吉爾（Virgil）的史詩《埃涅阿斯紀》（Aeneid，文本中記為 INIAD），並將其與荷馬（Homer）的《伊利亞德》和《奧德賽》進行了對比，最後為接下來即將學習的但丁《神曲》作了思想上的鋪墊。

以下為文本核心內容的深度摘要與解析：

一、 核心哲學對立：荷馬的「愛」與維吉爾的「虔誠」

講者首先定調，維吉爾在文學與哲學上是「反荷馬」（anti-Homer）的代表

。

荷馬的宇宙觀：在荷馬的史詩中，「愛」是宇宙的核心與統一力量，它賦予人類生命、目標與希望

。以《奧德賽》為例，奧德修斯（Odysseus）之所以能從戰爭的創傷與PTSD中重獲新生，是因為他選擇了回歸家庭與尋找愛

。愛賦予了他力量，正如他拒絕了女神卡呂普索（Calypso）的永生，選擇回到妻子潘妮洛普（Penelope）身邊，因為「愛就是心之所在」

。

維吉爾的宇宙觀：相反地，維吉爾認為宇宙的中心力量是「虔誠」（Piety），即對神明和父親的絕對服從

。在《埃涅阿斯紀》中，愛與虔誠被塑造成截然對立、互相競爭的力量，你無法在擁有愛的同時保持虔誠

。人類的責任就是盲從神明的計畫，而主角埃涅阿斯（Aeneas）的神聖使命便是建立羅馬帝國

。

二、 迦太基的悲劇：情感與責任的衝突

故事中段，埃涅阿斯遭遇海難，流落到迦太基（Carthage），並與女王狄多（Dido）相愛

。這段關係完美體現了史詩中情感與神聖使命的衝突。

神明的干預：反對羅馬崛起的茱諾（Juno）促成了兩人的結合，但眾神之王朱庇特（Jupiter）發現後，立刻派遣墨丘利（Mercury）下凡，嚴厲斥責埃涅阿斯忘記了自己的職責與兒子阿斯卡尼俄斯（Ascanius）的未來，命令他立刻啟航前往義大利

。

埃涅阿斯的冷酷反應：與荷馬筆下充滿情感掙扎的英雄不同，埃涅阿斯對神明的警告感到極度恐懼，他唯一的念頭就是服從神明、拋棄愛情

。他展現出極度缺乏人性的特質，甚至決定不告而別，像個「機器人」或執行任務的特務一樣，隨時準備開溜

。

狄多的崩潰與狂熱：狄多察覺了埃涅阿斯的背叛後，陷入了如同「酒神巴克斯（Bacchus）狂歡」般的瘋狂與憤怒

。講者在此解釋了希臘文化中的兩種創造力：阿波羅（Apollo）代表理性的創造，而巴克斯代表情感、狂熱的創造

。維吉爾藉此暗示，放任情感（巴克斯的一面）是最糟糕的，因為它會將人引向瘋狂

。狄多痛苦地控訴埃涅阿斯的無情，強調自己為了這段愛失去了榮譽、王國，甚至面臨周遭軍閥的威脅

。她懇求埃涅阿斯顧念他們曾經的誓言，但這與《奧德賽》中夫妻重逢時誓言的堅不可摧形成了強烈的諷刺與對比

。

任務高於一切：面對狄多的哀求，埃涅阿斯沒有展現任何憐憫

。他冷酷地辯稱兩人並未正式結婚，並表示如果可以選擇，他寧願回到特洛伊戰死；但現在為了追求權力與帝國，他必須前往義大利

。對他而言，權力才是生存的目的，愛只是絆腳石，必須被拋棄

。

三、 《埃涅阿斯紀》作為羅馬的政治宣傳與歷史顛覆

講者強調，《埃涅阿斯紀》本質上是一部政治宣傳作品（Political Propaganda）

。

合理化歷史上的暴行：羅馬與迦太基曾為爭奪地中海霸權進行了長達百年的戰爭，最終羅馬徹底摧毀了迦太基文明

。維吉爾透過虛構狄多的故事，將這場殘酷的毀滅合理化。史詩中，心碎的狄多在自殺前對埃涅阿斯發下了毒誓，詛咒兩國的子孫將陷入「無盡的戰爭」（Endless war）

。這暗示羅馬摧毀迦太基並非出於野蠻，而是因為迦太基人背負著復仇的詛咒，羅馬是迫不得已而戰

。

顛覆歷史上的狄多：在迦太基的真實歷史傳說中，狄多是一位為了保護人民自由、拒絕當地軍閥逼婚而壯烈自殺的偉大女王，她的犧牲贏得了人民與敵人的尊重

。然而，維吉爾卻將歷史顛覆，把狄多描寫成一個被愛毒害靈魂的女人，她的愛不僅毀了自己，還將她的人民奴役於無盡的復仇任務中

。這完全是為了服務羅馬政治目的而對歷史的篡改

。

四、 史詩的殘酷結局：完美的「機器人」誕生

演講的最後部分聚焦於史詩的結局，維吉爾在這裡對荷馬的《伊利亞德》進行了徹底的顛覆

。

與《伊利亞德》的對比：在《伊利亞德》結尾，阿基里斯（Achilles）殺死了赫克特（Hector）為友報仇，但他並未獲得榮耀，反而陷入深深的憂鬱；直到他與赫克特的父親普里阿摩斯（Priam）抱頭痛哭、互相寬恕，愛與憐憫才拯救了他的靈魂

。

埃涅阿斯對決圖爾努斯（Turnus）：在《埃涅阿斯紀》的結尾，埃涅阿斯擊敗了義大利本土的將領圖爾努斯

。倒地的圖爾努斯哀求饒命，他使用的台詞甚至直接「抄襲」了《伊利亞德》中普里阿摩斯的懇求（「想想你年邁的父親」）

。

真正的「頓悟」（Epiphany）：面對敵人的哀求，埃涅阿斯一度心生憐憫想要放過他

。但當他看到圖爾努斯肩上佩戴著自己戰死好友帕拉斯（Pallas）的劍帶時，瞬間狂怒，殘忍地一劍刺穿了圖爾努斯的心臟，史詩戛然而止

。

許多學者認為這個結局過於突兀，缺乏傳統文學中主角自我反省與昇華的「頓悟」或「淨化」（Catharsis）

。但講者指出，這正是學者們誤讀了這部政治宣傳作品

。埃涅阿斯的「頓悟」在於：他終於徹底殺死了自己的人性。過去，每當他偏離任務或流露情感時（例如想留在特洛伊或迦太基），都需要神明親自出面干預糾正

。但在這最後一刻，當他心中生出人類的憐憫之心時，他主動壓抑了它

。他意識到自己必須拋棄所有情感、憐憫與靈魂，才能成為完美服從神明意志的士兵

。維吉爾正是要透過這部作品對讀者進行洗腦，讓人們在背誦史詩的過程中，逐漸剝除使人軟弱的人性，變成如同埃涅阿斯一般服從統治的「機器人」

。

五、 關於愛的終極辯證與但丁的啟蒙

在問答環節中，有學生提出質疑：埃涅阿斯為了替好友帕拉斯報仇而殺人，這難道不是出於「愛」的情感嗎？

功利教育 vs. 愛的教育：講者藉此點出了現代教育的盲點。學生們接受的是「功利與服從」的教育，因此會認為「因為愛朋友，所以要殺死仇人」是理所當然的邏輯

。

愛的真諦：講者深刻地指出，真正的愛是純粹的善。如果你真的愛一個人，你絕不會用對方的記憶作為作惡或殺人的藉口

。真正的愛帶來的是寬恕，而不是將記憶轉化為仇恨與暴力

。如果用暴力來復仇，那意味著你其實並不真正懂愛

。這是一個非常難以理解的概念，因為我們的大腦已經被體制「洗腦」了

。

通往《神曲》的橋樑：《埃涅阿斯紀》塑造的羅馬帝國後來演變為統治歐洲千年的天主教會，帶來了長達千年的思想停滯與盲從（黑暗時代）

。在這個時代，所有菁英都在維吉爾的視角下看待世界

。而要摧毀這個壓抑人類想像力的帝國，必須等待但丁（Dante）的出現

。講者預告，課程接下來將逐字逐句地精讀但丁的《神曲》（Divine Comedy），這部不僅是詩歌更是哲學的偉大巨作，將會徹底擊碎羅馬帝國的舊有框架，並向世人揭示「上帝即是愛」的永恆真理

。

### **@vanessai405** | 👍 1 likes
[View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=YXTRlvFIRt8&lc=UgwoKZmz3-3eRriMsFB4AaABAg)

I don't see as something that takes a lifetime to understand, as though it's ultimately subjective. 

But I don’t think that’s because the text has no centre. I think it’s because Professor Jiang is trying to read something unified with a lens that still splits things apart. 
You can see it in the tension: on one hand, everything gets mapped through strategy, incentives, game theory & how people behave under pressure. On the other, he knows Dante is pointing to something deeper, ordered & real. 

But those two don’t fully reconcile where he’s standing. So the work stays open-ended because the frame can’t quite hold what it’s looking at. It can track distortion, patterns, misalignment, brilliantly. But it can’t fully account for what reorders them. That's the part Dante is actually pointing to.

Also, I'm not sure Virgil is the anti-Homer. That feels like reading a transition as an opposition. Homer shows a world where everything is in motion but not yet unified. Virgil tightens that into duty, piety, structure, but it's still external & still something imposed.

Dante is the real break, because once love becomes the organising force, obedience isn't the centre anymore.

I don't believe the real contrast is Homer vs Virgil, but force - then duty. Then something that actually holds both.

Hope this all makes sense.

---

Dante wrote a work titled On World-Government.

This work contradicts the idea that Dante opposed the Roman Empire.

I will list some of the title headings of book two to illustrate this point:



Book II: The Roman World Rule was Acquired by Right

 1. Both human reason and divine authority show that the Roman Empire existed by right

 2. The will of God is the basis or right

 3. The Roman people was the noblest

 4. Miracles attest God''s care for Rome

 5. Roman rule was for the common good

 6. The Roman capacity to rule proves that its rule was a natural order

 9. Rome won world-rule by ordeal, and hence by right

 12. Rome's divine authority is shown also by Christ's submission to Roman law

Could we infer that Shakespeare wrote Hamlet as a man caught between emulating Odysseus (love) and Aeneus (power)? When Claudius kills Hamlet's father for power, he makes Hamlet slowly go mad with revenge (wanting power). When Hamlet truly becomes like Aeneus, choosing power, he leaves Ophelia, who kills herself, just like Dido. Interesting also that it's Hamlet killing Ophelia's father Polonuis which really sends him over the edge. He fully rejects love in favor of power.
EDIT: Omg also- Hamlet's dad comes as a ghost to tell him to avenge his death. This is similar to how Aeneus is instructed by the gods and becomes pious at the end by killing for them.

Monotheist is one authority and a hierarchy of divinity, while polytheism is more accommodating to many beliefs. When describing the divine in monotheist world view it’s vagueness lends way to essentially the same concept as polytheism, everything is interconnected, god is everywhere, and so god is in me as it is in you; however this isn’t the message of those of religious authority. Preachers and disciples of the church are more attuned to gods word, even though in a sense there should be truth in all of us. Religious authority and conforming to this framing disconnects (hu)man from god, yet the idea of Jesus is to bridge that connection of god with man. It is the idea of Paul the apostle persecuting the first Christian’s to believe wholly that Jesus is the one and only god to cripple the religious revolution that Jesus should have brought upon the world, but the manipulation and control of religion is what has maintained the monotheistic hegemony

OK, so 'Professor' Jiang is a persona adopted for the channel.  I don't think people would have a problem at all, with that clarification.  In fact, Jiang invites people to scrutinise him and his message (Am I brainwashing you - is dissing Aeneas - weak man programming?) I always check out anybody that seems to be a 'guru' - and still think that Jiang's work is brilliant, and that he's an incredible polymath - but truth is important and it's wrong to call people 'haters' if they are respectful.  Rejecting information based on 'feelings' is a massive trap.  

Onto the book though, I found the Aeneid to be an incredible page turner - in a way that an ancient book rarely is.  I 'waded' though the Iliad, and the Odyssey a bit - I don't know about 'love' though, that sacrifice of Iphigenia was rank, and was Dido's willingness to sacrifice masses of humanity due to an emotional mess.  Dido wanted to sleep with 'Mars' and paid the price - the ending is indeed horrible, but it ensures that the book sticks with you - in fact Aeneas pretty much assumes the god form of Mars at the end of the Aeneid.

### **@Horsicorn**
[View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=YXTRlvFIRt8&lc=Ugw3VrzQtZftc_yIFTd4AaABAg)

The Aeneid isn't the masterpiece that the Iliad is, but this is such a bullshit, biased misrepresentation of Virgil's work. E.g. 20:30: the original Latin for "torment" is "curam"--most commonly translated as "care," very far from the "hatred" that Jiang invents to push his narrative. Other translations translate that passage as, "he smothered the pain deep within his heart." Jiang is really stretching the truth to portray the Aeneid as "inhuman" when in reality there's little to support that. Even the characterization of the Aeneid as Augustan propaganda is still debated among historians--there are many who argue that, while Augustus was the one commissioned the work from Virgil, Virgil was able to sneak in many criticisms of empire into the work, or that he never actually read aloud the entirety of the work to Augustus in court.
